#2294. La Stampa - Juve, Conte aspetta solo te. C'era un certo Inzaghi in pole ma ora...

La Stampa - Juve, Conte aspetta solo te. C'era un certo Inzaghi in pole ma ora...

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Certi amori non finiscono, fanno giri immensi e poi ritornano. E l'amore più grande di Antonio Conte è stato la Juventus. Così non dovrebbero neanche sorprendere più di tanto le parole dell'allenatore arrivate nelle scorse ore: «Prima o poi quel cerchio dovremo chiuderlo. Non so se tra uno, dieci, quindici anni ma so di aver lasciato qualcosa alla Juve».

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

Le parole arrivano in un momento in cui la Juve tentenna date le difficoltà del campo. Fino a poche settimane fa si parlava di esonero per Spalletti, tutto rientrato prima della pausa. Ma il fantasma di Conte aleggia sulla panchina bianconera. Le parole di ieri erano chiare e come spiega La Stampa hanno lasciato intendere che l'allenatore sta solo aspettando la telefonata che potrebbe portare alla svolta da parte della società bianconera. Conte ha spiegato anche che era stato vicino al ritorno a Torino prima di firmare per l'Inter e poi fu chiamato Sarri. Ed era stato vicino al ritorno anche prima dell'arrivo sulla panchina bianconera dell'ex nerazzurro Thiago Motta. Si parlava di lui e del suo possibile approdo al Manchester United. Il club inglese nel frattempo ha scelto Pochettino. E così Conte è tornato prepotentemente ad aleggiare sulla Juve.

"E la candidatura dell’uomo preferito dal popolo bianconero, in questa fase, non farà altro che aumentare la pressione. Sia su Spalletti che su John Elkann e Giovanni Carnevali, perché quando arriverà il momento di decidere un cambio in panchina, sarà sempre più difficile ignorare una disponibilità resa così palese di Conte al ritorno. Sia che la posizione di Spalletti torni in bilico da qui alla sosta di Natale, sia che si parli di un cambiamento a fine stagione (in pole sembrava esserci un certo Simone Inzaghi), o ancora nella remota ipotesi che si aspetti la naturale scadenza di contratto fissata al 30 giugno 2028. Non ha fretta Conte, il mercato di sicuro non gli manca e una stagione di decompressione l’aveva ampiamente messa in preventivo", si legge sul quotidiano torinese a proposito delle parole di Conte e a proposito di come hanno lasciato un'impronta nell'ambiente bianconero.

#2292. Zabatta: “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport, una grande festa per tutta la Campania”

Zabatta: “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport, una grande festa per tutta la Campania”

Presentata in Consiglio regionale l’iniziativa del 22 novembre alla Mostra d’Oltremare. Una giornata ad ingresso libero tra campioni, scuole, associazioni e cittadini.

Una giornata per riunire il movimento sportivo campano, valorizzarne i protagonisti e promuovere una pratica sportiva sempre più accessibile e inclusiva. È “Verso gli Stati Generali dello Sport 2026”, l’iniziativa promossa dall’Assessorato allo Sport della Regione Campania, presentata questa mattina nella Sala Caduti di Nassiriya del Consiglio regionale.

L’appuntamento è per domenica 22 novembre alla Mostra d’Oltremare, Padiglione 3B , con incontri, premiazioni, attività dimostrative e occasioni di confronto tra istituzioni, federazioni, associazioni, scuole, atleti e cittadini.

Alla conferenza stampa hanno partecipato l’assessora regionale allo Sport Fiorella Zabatta, il presidente del Consiglio regionale Massimiliano Manfredi, Sandro Cuomo, campione olimpico nella spada a squadre ad Atlanta 1996, e l’atleta paralimpica Angela Procida.

“Vogliamo che sia una grande festa dello sport campano, un’occasione per dare spazio a ogni disciplina, a ogni talento e a ogni storia. Lo sport è passione, sacrificio e inclusione, una palestra di vita che unisce generazioni e crea comunità. Con questa iniziativa, unica nel suo genere in Campania, vogliamo riunire chi vive lo sport ogni giorno: dagli atleti alle associazioni, dalle scuole alle federazioni, fino alle famiglie. Il nostro obiettivo è costruire uno sport davvero accessibile, perché non deve essere un privilegio legato alle possibilità economiche, ma un diritto e un’opportunità per tutti”, ha detto l’ assessora regionale allo Sport Fiorella Zabatta.

”Gli Stati generali dello sport devono segnare l’avvio di una nuova stagione di impegno e di politiche concrete per la Campania. Lo sport non è un lusso né un privilegio, ma un diritto e un fondamentale strumento di crescita, salute, educazione e inclusione sociale. La nostra sfida è garantire a ogni cittadino, indipendentemente dal territorio in cui vive o dalle proprie condizioni economiche e personali, la possibilità di praticarlo. Servono investimenti negli impianti, sostegno alle associazioni e alle società sportive e una particolare attenzione ai giovani, alle persone con disabilità e alle fasce più fragili. Il Consiglio regionale è pronto a fare la propria parte: dobbiamo trasformare le tante energie del mondo sportivo in una visione comune e in azioni concrete. Perché investire nello sport significa investire nel futuro, nella coesione e nella qualità della vita della nostra comunità. La Campania ha bisogno di una politica dello sport che non lasci indietro nessuno”, ha aggiunto il presidente del Consiglio regionale Massimiliano Manfredi.

Il programma del 22 novembre inizia alle 9.30 von l’accoglienza dei partecipanti; alle 10.30 apriranno gli stand delle Federazioni sportive campane. Alle 11 il convegno “Sport e scuola: educazione, inclusione e benessere” darà il via alla sessione mattutina, che proseguirà con le premiazioni di “Napoli Capitale Europea dello Sport” e i riconoscimenti alle scuole, alle associazioni e alle realtà che si sono distinte nella promozione della pratica sportiva.

Alle 18 si terrà il convegno “Presente e Futuro dello Sport in Campania”, seguito dalle premiazioni della Presidenza del Consiglio regionale e dai riconoscimenti ai grandi protagonisti dello sport, tra cui la campionessa olimpica di ginnastica artistica Manila Esposito, altri atleti e rappresentanti delle Federazioni sportive.

Per tutta la giornata sarà possibile visitare gli stand, conoscere e provare diverse discipline sportive e partecipare alle attività dimostrative. Alle 20 è previsto lo show cooking “La dieta dello sportivo”.

L’iniziativa è organizzata dall’Assessorato allo Sport della Regione Campania, con la partecipazione del CONI Campania, del Comitato Italiano Paralimpico (CIP), di Sport e Salute e di Padelness e Sport, con il supporto operativo di Sviluppo Campania. L’ingresso è libero .

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2288. SCOTLAND 2 NORTH MACEDONIA 1: John McGinn at the double to spare the blushes of Pocognoli's shaky Scots on skipper Robertson's big night

By JOHN MCGARRY, SCOTTISH CHIEF SPORTS FEATURE WRITER

Published: 17:01 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 17:11 EST, 13 November 2026

On the night when the nation looked over its shoulder and celebrated the past, the here and now for Scotland looked distinctly uncertain.

Coming 40 years and a day after his friend, Sir Kenny Dalglish, pulled on a Scotland jersey for the 102nd and final time, Andy Robertson joined the great man on a mark which, for years, seemed beyond the reach of mere mortals.

By time up, history had mercifully repeated itself at Hampden. Back in the day, Dalglish, by then player-manager at Anfield, sailed into the international sunset on the back of a routine 3-0 win over Luxembourg.

Facing another opponent of modest ability, Robertson’s red letter day didn’t go quite as smoothly. Yet, when the Tottenham man is old and grey and reflecting on the night he created history, the annals will at least record a slender victory.

While his side did just about enough to prevail here, this was another fraught night for Sebastien Pocognoli.

You can talk about the need for patience under the new manager until the cows come home. But North Macedonia are 71st in FIFA’s rankings. Scotland are 43rd.

John McGinn celebrates in trademark fashion after netting Scotland's winner from the spot

At Hampden Park, in front of an expectant crowd, Scotland should be putting them to the sword with something to spare.

Yet, for long enough, the Belgian’s new-look side were flat, predictable and plodding. It said much that it took the intervention of VAR for them to get the penalty which would ultimately lead to three points being accrued.

John McGinn’s second half double saved the Belgian’s blushes and truthfully there was little else to get the Tartan Army off their seats.

Too many big-name players scarcely merited pass marks. Too few looked comfortable in the system the manager is trying to bed in.

If it was a step in the right direction, it was a small one. After five games under the affable Belgian, the progress has certainly not been startling.

While it goes without saying that beating the Swiss in St Gallen on Monday will be an inordinately tougher task, having some hope of moving up a place and securing a kinder draw for the Euros is still better than no hope.

Regardless of what pot they end up in when the draw is made in Belfast next month, a radical improvement will certainly be required across the board if they are to stand any chance of qualifying for the tournament via the front door.

As well as the achievements of Robertson and Dalglish, the career of Craig Gordon was acknowledged before the match with the 84-times capped keeper deservedly afforded a warm hand by the Tartan Army.

McGinn slots home his penalty after Ben Gannon-Doak had been brought down in the area

There were absolutely no excuses for Scotland here. North Macedonia were a tier-two team in name only. They arrived at Hampden having not won in 12 matches and not scored in eight.

Another Pocognoli game saw another back-three combination. This time Luke Graham was selected in the centre of the unit with Ryan Porteous and Kieran Tierney deployed either side of the Stoke man in what were their first appearances under the Belgian.

Robertson and Ben Gannon-Doak were tasked with providing the width in the 3-4-3. Scott McTominay slotted back into an advanced role beside John McGinn with the returning Che Adams asked to lead the line.

For all there was a healthy crowd in excess of 40,000 inside Hampden, the atmosphere was subdued. In the four Nations League games to date under the new manager, the side hadn’t exactly given the supporters much to feed off.

Despite some big guns returning, Scotland made a laboured start to their night’s work. It said much that Graham, on his second appearance, was busier than any defender in white.

The first opening came as a result of an opposition error. John McGinn found Billy Gilmour who fed the overlapping Robertson. The skipper’s cross was well out of Adams’ reach.

Sebastien Pocognoli got his first Hampden win as Scotland boss but it was a nervy night

Although Ljupche Doriev worried Angus Gunn with a curling effort, Scotland began to exert some degree of control on the match. Lewis Ferguson kept the visitors hemmed in with a header into McTominay’s feet. The Napoli man saw his shot on the spin well saved by Stole Dimitrievski.

Pocognoli was frustrated when Slovakian referee Michal Ocenas blew for a foul on McTominay, his justifiable gripe being that playing an advantage would have seen Robertson have a crack at goal.

Dimitrievski had a decent night. Although Adams’ flashing header from McGinn’s corner took a deflection, a reflex save at his near post added to Scotland’s frustration.

North Macedonia were a spirited but ordinary side. They still had enough to thwart Pocognoli’s men.

Gannon-Doak saw plenty of the ball down the right, yet the end product often wasn’t there. The Bournemouth man did tee up Billy Gilmour for one dangerous cross. McTominay tried to replicate his iconic overhead kick against Denmark nearly a year ago. This time, his aim wasn’t true.

You could hardly blame Pocognoli’s star man for failing to find the net on that occasion.

Scott McTominay attempts an overhead spectacular a year on from THAT goal at Hampden

That wasn’t the case a few moments later when Adams slipped him in. Dimitrievski saved McTominay’s strike with an outstretched leg when he should have been picking the ball out of his net.

Adams had just blazed a strike high over the top while one-on-one with the keeper although the offside flag saved his blushes.

The visitors came closer to edging ahead when Eljif Elmas played the pass of the match over Scotland’s static defence to pick out Ezgjan Alioski. His lay-off was smashed into the side net by Enis Bardhi.

A wholly uninspiring first-half concluded with Robertson advancing up the left and launching a cross halfway to Castlemilk. This was not going well.

Pocognoli waited on the edge of the pitch for his players returning, his enthusiastic applause clearly audible in the arena.

His hopes of a breakthrough at the outset of the second period were dashed in extraordinary fashion. Adams did well to chase a ball down to the line and cut it back. McTominay and his marker couldn’t connect, leaving Gilmour the simple task of firing into an unguarded net. Leaning back, his failure to even hit the target was unfathomable.

Elmin Rastoder celebrates after silencing Hampden with a leveller for North Macedonia

Within seconds, the sense of relief inside Hampden was palpable as the opener finally arrived. Porteous got enough distance on his clearing header for Adams to barge Visar Musliu out of the way.

McGinn was alert to the situation and burst into the box. His crisp strike finally had the beating of the goalkeeper.

It should have settled Pocognoli’s side and paved the way for a comfortable result. Instead, they were pegged back within three minutes.

It was all just so simple and so avoidable. Bardhi’s whipped corner only needed McTominay to outjump his man. Flat-footed as the delivery arrived, he could only watch on in horror as Elmin Rastoder’s flashing header beat Angus Gunn.

Although Gilmour worked the keeper with a low strike, North Macedonia also sniffed a second goal. Time and time again, they got into pockets of space on the flanks and fired in dangerous crosses.

Adams made way for Oli McBurnie, the Hull man desperately hoping for his first goal on his 21st international appearance. He had a gilt-edged chance to get off the mark when rolled in by McGinn, but selflessly passed to the overlapping Gannon-Doak.

Scotland move on to their final game away in Switzerland as they look to finish on a high note

The winger appeared to take a heavy touch which allowed the keeper to rescue the situation. VAR, however, spotted that Dimitrievski had impeded him. Penalty.

McGinn did the necessary from 12 yards, smashing the ball straight down the middle for his 24th international goal.

North Macedonia, to their credit, kept going for it. They sensed a fragility in Scotland. Everyone in the ground could.

McGinn’s calm conversion proved to be just about enough to get the job done. But it’s fair to suggest that we are still awaiting lift-off in the new era.

SCOTLAND (3-4-1-2): Gunn; Porteous, Graham, Tierney; Gannon-Doak (Hickey 76), Ferguson, Gilmour (Christie 76), Robertson; McGinn; Adams (McBurnie 67), McTominay. Booked: Ferguson. Manager: Sebastien Pocognoli.

NORTH MACEDONIA (4-2-3-1): Dimitrievski; Ilievski, Mladenovski, Musliu, Alioski; Gashtarov (Elezi 64), Elmas (Stankovski 78); Churlinov (Maleski 64), Bardhi, Doriev (Nikolov 85); Rastoder. Booked: None. Manager: Goce Sedloski.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Aurelio De Laurentiis ha definito l’accordo per l’acquisto dei terreni dove sorgerà il nuovo centro sportivo. L’appuntamento sarebbe stato fissato mercoledì 18 novembre alle ore 12 nello studio notarile a Chiaia in via dei Mille di Francesco Coppa. Si tratta di un acquisto preliminare, che riguarderebbe suoli per 13 ettari, anche se in futuro nell’affare potrebbero rientrare almeno altri cinque ettari di terreni. Con le firme sui contratti, il presidente e la societ diventeranno ufficialmente proprietari di quasi 13 ettari in località Pozzo Nuovo a Qualiano. Le trattative sono terminate, entro gennaio 2027 inizieranno anche i lavori per la trasformazione dell’area. Il nuovo training center potrebbe essere operativo entro 12 mesi. Perché ora il Napoli ha fretta di spostarsi nella sua nuova casa: e quindi, verrà data la priorità alla realizzazione di (almeno) tre campi di calcio necessari per gli allenamenti della prima squadra, gli spogliatoi, le palestre, le piscine, le aree dei massaggi e quelle per gli uffici. E di una palazzina per il ristoro. Poi, solo in un secondo momento, si aprirà il cantiere per la foresteria e per la realizzazione di un convitto per i giovani. Lo scrive Il Corriere del Mezzogiorno

“Serie A in corso? Mi auguravo la ricerca di un calcio offensivo, anche a discapito di qualche errore difensivo. Bisogna esplorare credendo. C’è il Como, c’è il Frosinone, c’è la Lazio. Vogliamo fare i conti dei soldi dalle big? La chiave è quanto spendi, senza sprecare però. Ora tu vorresti Paz, Ramon, Da Cunha, Butez, adesso. Prima no, quindi sono le competenze, se vedono solo loro. Sono andato a vedere Como-Torino, sono entrato in spogliatoio, aveva una lucidità che non può esistere dopo una partita. Dopo una gara sono tutti stravolti. Una seduta totale di analisi del match, lucidissimo, appena finita la gara”.

#2282. De Winter e la vita da papà: "Cambiata molto. Quando sei al lavoro, pensi alla figlia, vuoi tornare a casa più presto"

Koni De Winter, difensore del Milan, è stato intervistato da Claudio Raimondi di SportMediaset a margine del Meet&Greet al Milan Store di San Babila. Si riporta di seguito un estratto delle sue dichiarazioni.

Sei diventato da pochissimo papà di una bellissima bambina. Come sono le notti da papà?

"Le notti dico bene, la mia fidanzata sta facendo un gran lavoro, quindi per me è molto, cioè mi alzo a volte, però è molto più tranquillo. Penso che per lei sia più dura".

E quanto è cambiata la vita con l'arrivo di una figlia?

"È cambiata, molto, cioè non so come spiegare. Quando sei al lavoro, pensi alla figlia, vuoi tornare a casa più presto, la vuoi tenere in braccio, la vuoi, cioè hai più momenti belli, però è difficile da spiegare. Non so come spiegare".

#2280. Adani: "Crociata contro Allegri? Non è così, vi spiego. E a De Zerbi ho detto che..."

Adani: "Crociata contro Allegri? Non è così, vi spiego. E a De Zerbi ho detto che..."

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Al podcast Passa dal BSMT di Gianluca Gazzoli, l’ex difensore Lele Adani ha parlato così del gesto 'facciamo calcio' e di Massimiliano Allegri.

"Allegri consapevole del gesto “facciamo calcio”? Il gesto è nato semplicemente in una camera, l’equivalente dell’idratazione nella camera d’hotel. Lì ero a Francoforte e parlavo dopo una partita dove la Slovacchia batte il Belgio. A un certo punto parlo di Lobotka e dico che fa calcio. E piace, ma il gesto di fare calcio è una cosa che è vecchia come la Bibbia, da sempre, è un gesto. Ma se lo metti lì e non lo guardi, passa come scontato. Per quello mi è piaciuto quando tanti l’hanno preso. È legato al calcio giocato. Se Max lo fa riferendosi ai suoi, lo fa per dire a loro di tenere palla. Poi ci metto il video e le parole giuste, ma bisogna lavorarci. L’attitudine è unire, non separare. Che sia Allegri, Spalletti, Italiano, De Zerbi, Mancini, se vedo un percorso nobile io lo riconosco. Non ho nessun motivo personale per favorire uno o penalizzare un altro. Lo rivendico questo.

Sono stato molto critico con De Zerbi negli ultimi mesi, lui è mio fratello. Gli ho detto che il secondo anno a Marsiglia ha fallito e al Tottenham all’inizio ho detto che è stato terrificante, ho esagerato. Io devo premiare Allegri se fa bene e ci avevo sperato io. Lo scorso anno al Milan dicevo che vedevo qualcosa, però se poi è una speranza e non accade, non sarei felice io. Crociata contro Max? Non è così. Molti dicono che sia l’artefice della nuova comunicazione come De Zerbi lo è in campo. De Zerbi nuovo guru del calcio giocato e quello della nuova comunicazione in nome del ‘bel gioco’ che è un concetto vuoto, questo dicono”.

#2279. Inside Boreham Wood, the only unbeaten team in England's top seven tiers: Boss LUKE GARRARD explains how play-off heartache and a 'genius' owner are fuelling his swashbuckling side's promotion bid

Published: 21:00 EST, 12 November 2026 | Updated: 17:17 EST, 13 November 2026

Of 252 clubs in the top seven tiers of English football, the last one unbeaten can be found in the pristine confines of Meadow Park, yards from the studios where the Indiana Jones adventures were filmed.

Boreham Wood are on their own crusade. And the Holy Grail is a long-coveted place in the EFL for the runaway leaders of the National League. They have 44 points from 16 games with 44 goals and are 12 points clear ahead of a trip to Yeovil on Saturday, finding success with a distinct passing style.

In a remarkable 4-3 comeback win against Hartlepool earlier this month, Luke Garrard's team made 1,022 passes, beating the Premier League record of 1,015 set by Manchester City against Swansea in April 2018.

The electric start to this campaign is, according to Garrard, fuelled by the lingering pain of defeat in the play-off final at Wembley in May when they conceded an equaliser in the seventh minute of stoppage time and lost on penalties.

'That broke me mentally for four or five days,' the Wood boss tells Daily Mail Sport. 'I reflected hugely and had a long, hard look at myself because I could not walk back for the first day of pre-season without the energy and heart for the job.

'I spoke to the owners and the staff about the first meeting I wanted to have when we came back in. It involved everyone at the club, and it was a message to tell all of them that as much as it hurt us, we needed to use the hurt as our fuel and our driving force.

'We got quite lucky getting some results on the board quite quickly and that builds momentum and belief.'

National League leaders Boreham Wood, under the guidance of their manager Luke Garrard, are the last unbeaten team in the top seven tiers of English football

Boreham Wood are a slick operation, skilfully run despite crowds seldom breaking 1,500. They share Meadow Park with Arsenal Women and Arsenal Under 21s and U18s

Boreham Wood are a slick operation, skilfully run despite crowds seldom breaking 1,500 by chairman Danny Hunter and his son Charlie, who is the sporting director and in charge of recruitment strategy. They are punching above their weight with a mid-table budget.

Hunter senior is a former props master in the film industry who worked at the nearby Elstree Studios among others, and whose father Mickey was a double promotion winning manager of club in the 1970s.

He took control in 1999 and has overseen investment of more than £14million and the rise from the depths of the Isthmian League to the brink of the EFL, forging effective relationships with local authorities and Premier League neighbours Arsenal, who use Meadow Park for their Women, Under-21 and Under-18 teams.

Arsenal ad boards surround an immaculate pitch, and work is underway at the venue on a £4m project to create a covered South Stand terrace incorporating improved 'Fan Zone' style facilities for away fans and a new first-team building.

Garrard's team train in the mornings on the artificial pitches beside the stadium, which are available for community use in the evenings and home in the afternoons to the club's academy.

Manchester City's Iliman Ndiaye and Hull's Sorba Thomas were both nurtured as young players at Boreham Wood. So was Abdul Abdulmalik, a 23-year-old winger signed after his release from Millwall in 2024 and sold to Djurgardens in Sweden for £1.5m in August.

The Abdulmalik fee shattered the non-League record set when Fleetwood Town sold Jamie Vardy to Leicester in 2012.

There was an untidy episode towards the end of the transfer window when Abdulmalik went public to plead for a move, but the Hunters secured an enviable price for their star and in another example of their good housekeeping have yet to reinvest the money in the team. It is still there as a war chest as they target promotion.

Abdul Abdulmalik, a 23-year-old winger signed after his release from Millwall in 2024, was sold to Djurgardens in Sweden for £1.5million in August

The club is run by chairman Danny Hunter (pictured) and his son Charlie, who is the sporting director. Hunter Snr is a former props master in the film industry

The windfall from Wood's famous FA Cup run in 2021-22 was invested into refitting the interiors of the ground including the dressing rooms and swish hospitality areas recognised among the best in non-League.

Meadow Park has a bar bedecked with souvenirs from those games and named in honour of Mark Ricketts, scorer of the goal to beat Bournemouth, who were at the time bound for promotion to the Premier League, and which clinched a date with Everton in the fifth round at Goodison Park.

'At no stage has the chairman given me any pressure to generate money from prize funds and cup runs,' says 41-year-old Garrard. 'It's only ever been about creating moments and memories.

'He (Hunter) is a Boreham Wood boy through and through and it's always been his greatest wish to lead the club into the EFL. He wants to leave a legacy. I adore him, I think he's a genius. We have a great working relationship. I do not take for granted the luxury of being manager here, especially after losing my job two years ago.'

Garrard has a long association with the club. First as a player, briefly in 2005 and then returning for a second spell four years later, a loan move from Wimbledon which became permanent and led him into his first coaching roles and the manager's job.

He took control in 2015 with the Wood in the top tier of non-League for the first time. Three times they reached the play-offs without promotion and there were many highs including Goodison before Garrard's tearful exit after relegation in April 2024, only for him to be back in charge by September.

During five months away, he turned down interest from one club because it took him too far from his family home and received two direct and sobering rejections from National League clubs. Feedback from the first told him he 'wasn't on the list and never would be' and from the second that he 'wasn't good enough' for them.

Perhaps it reveals some of football's inherent petty-mindedness towards Boreham Wood. They are perceived by some as upstarts with limited history and small crowds, putting the noses of 'bigger' clubs out of joint with the streamlined success of their business model and Hunter's backing, like a non-League version of Bournemouth or Brentford.

They have been known to charge scouts for tickets and charge away teams to broadcast, which some refuse to do on a point of principle. For a time, they had a reputation for pragmatic and unattractive football – but not any longer.

Garrard was rejected by two National League clubs and found himself back at Boreham Wood. 'The stars aligned,' as he puts it

Prolific striker Matt Rush has is fuelling Boreham Wood's promotion push

Garrard was probably entitled to have expected more interest in his services. Instead, he spent two days following the second of those rejections in earnest conversation with his wife about the need to look for work away from football when out of the blue came a call from Boreham Wood.

Within another two days he was back in his old job. 'The stars aligned,' as he puts it. 'It was the best team in the National South with the best players, the best facilities and the best budget. I had to go and deliver.'

And he did. With promotion back to the National League at the first attempt. Then the near-miss heartache of Wembley. Now this early-season surge thanks in part to the goals of striker Matt Rush. The Wood seem to have emerged stronger despite selling their most talented individual, which is a reassuring situation for any coach.

Unbeaten since April in the National League, they scored 25 goals in seven games in October and League One Wigan became the first team to beat them in any competition this season, in the FA Cup first round on Saturday.

It could yet prove a blessing in Boreham Wood's quest for the Holy Grail.

A sorpresa il nome di Matteo Renzi è stato accostato in queste ore alla Lazio. #2274. Andy's big night should have been so much more straightforward but that won't matter when he reflects on history made at Hampden

Published: 17:21 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 17:24 EST, 13 November 2026

If Andy Robertson’s first 101 caps for Scotland taught him anything, it’s that we have never been a nation to make life easy for ourselves. It’s in our DNA.

Why, therefore, would he have expected cap No102 to be any different?

This was meant to have been a celebration of one of Scotland’s greatest-ever servants on the night when he equalled Kenny Dalglish’s total appearances against the whipping boys of Nations League Group B1.

As the cliché goes, there’s no gimmes at this level. But that’s nonsense. This is exactly what Robertson’s special night should have been. A gimme.

The visitors hadn’t registered a goal yet in this campaign, never mind a point. They were rotten when Robertson and Co scored twice without reply in Skopje last month, and it was the same story for much of this forgettable contest.

The problem is that Scotland weren’t much better. In the first half, at least.

Andy Robertson joined Kenny Dalglish on 102 caps as Scotland saw off North Macedonia

Thankfully, they got the job done after the break to ensure it would be a night Robertson will look back on fondly after all.

He’s earned that too, even if his own individual performance didn’t quite hit the heights of so many that have gone before. But nobody will remember that.

Perhaps he knackered himself by covering what must have been around 10km in a small-sided kickabout during the warm-up. He may not have the same turn of pace in his arsenal that he possessed when he first broke into the international fold as a fresh-faced teenager. But, in terms of intensity, the 32-year-old is still an animal, in the nicest possible way. He hassled and harried every one of his team-mates throughout the pre-game stuff, barking out all sorts of instructions along the way.

In many ways, he is still the same standard-bearer he’s always been. It was a shame he couldn’t quite lead the way when the serious stuff got started.

Operating on the left-hand side of a four-man midfield, the Spurs man – as he does as frequently as ever – bombed up and down the flank all night. However, his deliveries from several promising positions weren’t quite up to scratch.

A couple hit the first man. A couple cleared the upright. None caused any sort of alarm in the North Macedonian backline.

Robertson was his customary energetic self as he bombed up and down the left-hand side

He was made to work harder than he would have liked at the other end too, as the visitors threatened to spoil the party on more than one occasion in the second 45.

In truth, he didn’t look particularly comfortable in Sebastien Pocognoli’s new-look side. Then again, few did.

The Belgian will be given plenty of time to really put his stamp on this Scotland team, but there’s no two ways about it. The early signs are uninspiring to say the least.

By the end, people got sick of the attritional style employed by Steve Clarke. Pocognoli arrived with a reputation for going with a more enterprising approach.

Really? It’s fair to say, the wait to see it in action goes on.

It’s not the time to panic. But you get the sense patience is already being stretched amongst some sections of the Tartan Army.

They weren’t shy in airing their frustrations last night. Scott McTominay was on the receiving end of a few grumbles, as was Ben Gannon-Doak who once again did so much right until the all-important crucial last bit.

Yes, he may have won the winning penalty which John McGinn duly slotted away, but let’s be honest, there was more than a touch of good fortune about it.

The Scotland skipper leads his side out at Hampden on a night he will remember forever

He should have burst the net when put clean through by Oli McBurnie long before keeper Stole Dimitrievski caught him near the byline.

The Bournemouth youngster is seen by many as undroppable, and there’s merit to that argument in that Pocognoli doesn’t have anyone else like him to call upon. However, he needs to improve the final part of his game to ensure he becomes Scotland’s main man in the years to come.

Had Scotland been a few goals to the good by the time McGinn stepped up to notch his second of the night, you suspect Robertson may have fancied taking the spot-kick himself.

Still, the record books will show that Robertson’s record-equalling night will ultimately go down as a triumph for all involved, even if it didn’t feel like it at the time.

#2272. Cosmi, squalifica per le parole a Butic: «Sei uno zingaro»

L’allenatore ha sostenuto di aver usato una frase diversa; il Giudice Sportivo ha ritenuto attendibili le testimonianze raccolte.

Tre mesi di squalifica e 10mila euro di ammenda per Serse Cosmi. Secondo quanto riportato da SportMediaset, Il Giudice Sportivo della Lega Pro ha sanzionato l’ex allenatore della Salernitana per un’espressione discriminatoria rivolta all’attaccante croato del Bari Karlo Butic durante la gara dello scorso 4 ottobre allo stadio Arechi. La decisione arriva dopo la conclusione dell’incarico di Cosmi sulla panchina granata.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

la sanzione resterà congelata fino al prossimo incarico ufficiale dell’allenatore.

L’episodio risale all’intervallo di Salernitana-Bari, partita poi vinta in rimonta per 2-1 dai campani. Nel primo tempo Butic aveva portato avanti il Bari trasformando un calcio di rigore. Aveva quindi festeggiato sotto la Curva Sud, settore del tifo granata.

L’esultanza non era piaciuta a Cosmi. Al duplice fischio, mentre le squadre rientravano negli spogliatoi, l’allora tecnico della Salernitana si era avvicinato all’attaccante per parlargli. Secondo la ricostruzione accolta dalla giustizia sportiva, gli avrebbe detto «Sei uno zingaro».

Butic avrebbe cercato di spiegare che il festeggiamento era una dedica alla compagna incinta. Cosmi, sempre secondo la ricostruzione dei fatti, avrebbe risposto: «Che me ne frega della tua ragazza».

La decisione è arrivata dopo gli accertamenti svolti dalla Procura Federale al termine della partita. L’arbitro, il primo assistente e il quarto ufficiale hanno dichiarato di non aver sentito le parole scambiate tra i due: si trovavano a circa venti metri di distanza. Un collaboratore della Procura Federale, che era invece a circa un metro da Cosmi e Butic, ha confermato di aver udito distintamente le parole denunciate dal calciatore del Bari.

Ascoltato durante le indagini, Cosmi non ha negato né il confronto con Butic né l’uso del termine contestato. Ha però sostenuto di aver pronunciato una frase diversa: «Ho detto a Butic: 'Non ti lamentare se poi ti chiamano zingaro'».

Il Giudice Sportivo non ha accolto questa versione. Ha ritenuto pienamente attendibili il racconto del calciatore e quello del collaboratore federale, qualificando l’espressione come discriminatoria per motivi di razza, nazionalità e origine etnica.

#2268. Kevin De Bruyne to leave Napoli 'the sooner the better', his wife announces: Man City legend, 35, desperate to quit his 'not nice existence' after rows with team-mates, bosses and fan issues

Published: 17:01 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 17:58 EST, 13 November 2026

Kevin De Bruyne's wife Michelle has opened up on her family's struggles in Naples and desire to move back home to Belgium one day.

De Bruyne, 35, joined Napoli in the summer of 2025 after leaving Manchester City following a legendary ten-year spell.

But things haven't gone to plan in Italy, with De Bruyne struggling for fitness in his first season, while he also clashed with former boss Antonio Conte.

The situation hasn't improved much this time around under Max Allegri as Napoli currently sit 10th in Serie A, and reports broke earlier this season that the Belgian midfielder was involved in a dressing room bust-up with team-mates.

And De Bruyne's wife Michele, who he married in 2017, reflected on the turbulence in an interview with Belgian magazine NINA back in September.

'Of course, we think about the future together,' he said. 'And increasingly often, we say to each other: "We are going to live here in Limburg. The sooner we get here, the better".'

Kevin De Bruyne's wife Michelle has opened up on her family's struggles living in Naples

De Bruyne moved to Napoli in the summer of 2025 after a glittering 10 years at Manchester City

Limburg is a province in Belgium, and although Michelle insisted that she thought Naples was a beautiful place to live with great weather and food, she spoke about the toll that life in Italy has had on her partner and their three children, Mason, Rome and Suri.

'Kevin leads a very restricted life in Napoli,' she added. 'It's not much fun for him. Besides, we can't do anything together as a family in the city. That's the other side of the coin, isn't it?

'In Manchester, it was different. Where we lived, people had seen us so often that it wasn’t special anymore. We could walk out the door there without a single photo being taken. In Naples, that is impossible.'

She later added: 'At a certain point, football is no longer the priority. You have achieved everything, done everything. Why would you then turn your children’s lives upside down?'

De Bruyne, who is one of the highest paid players in Italy, signed a two-year contract when he arrived at Napoli but it wasn't certain that he would make it into the second part of that deal in the summer.

Reflecting on his first year ahead of the World Cup, De Bruyne insisted he was 'happy' that Conte had departed.

'Obviously it was difficult for me because Conte has a very different vision of football to mine,' De Bruyne said.

'In truth, I never had the opportunity to play in my preferred position. In any case, I always tried to give my all for the team. We played very defensively. If you try to win every game by a one-goal margin with a 4-5-1 formation, you play a certain type of football.

De Bruyne signed a two-year deal at Napoli and is unlikely to extend his time in Italy

'At the start of the season, we sat back even deeper. Our centre-forward scored just 10 goals, so you know the statistics are not going to be great…'

He later added: 'For me, yes (I'm happy Conte has gone). As far as I am concerned, he was not obliged to stay. There were promises made last summer on the way that we would be playing, but at the end of the day not much of it happened.

'I found that a pity. Football needs to be enjoyable, and unfortunately, I found that aspect lacking.

'There’s no point beating around the bush, we have different visions of football. I never got to play in my position under Conte.'

Al podcast Passa dal BSMT di Gianluca Gazzoli, l’ex difensore Lele Adani ha parlato anche della fine della Bobo TV. “Qualcosa che mi manca del periodo della Bobo TV? Sono cose anche vecchie ormai, no? Fino a quel momento della rottura comunque lo ricordo e sono grato di averlo vissuto. Chiaramente da quando ci siamo separati, ma sono anche quasi contento che si sia fermato, dico che è meglio così.

La cosa importante della vita è che prima di tutto ti cresce. Io ne avrei fatto a meno sempre e credo tutti, ma ti cresce, ti forma, ti fa vedere le cose in un altro modo. Ma io non cambierei nulla. Io lo ritornerei a vivere il mio rapporto nello stesso modo, è stato troppo bello, ma meno male che si è fermato e sono molto più felice ora.

Riunirsi come gli Oasis? Dal mio punto di vista, credo che neanche se ne parlerà, è una cosa archiviata per sempre. Torno a dirlo: auguro il meglio io, non si vive di rancore per com’è andata, padre tempo opera sempre in silenzio. Ma sempre spererò che tutto vada per il meglio a tutti gli interpreti. Senza portare rancore, ma con la fermezza e la consapevolezza che non ci sarà più quel tempo”.

#2263. Costruttore occulto, duttilità e piedi buoni: ecco perché l'Inter vuole Provstgaard

Costruttore occulto, duttilità e piedi buoni: ecco perché l'Inter vuole Provstgaard

Installa la nostra App gratuita e accedi più velocemente alle notizie

Mentre navighi nell'app, scorri il dito da sinistra a destra dello schermo per tornare alle pagine precedenti

Scegli Fcinter1908.it come tuo sito preferito su Google: clicca qui

E' di queste ore la notizia, riferita dalla Gazzetta dello Sport, dell'interesse dell'Inter nei confronti del difensore della Lazio Oliver Provstgaard. La rosea aggiunge qualche dettaglio in più sui motivi che hanno spinto i nerazzurri a mettersi sulle tracce del danese classe 2003:

"Il radar dell’Inter è arrivato fino al costruttore “occulto” di Gattuso. Il centrale difensivo da cui partono la maggior parte delle azioni offensive della Lazio. Il modus operandi è il seguente: quando Oliver Provstgaard ha la sfera tra i piedi, lì davanti si muovono a seconda dello schema".

"Il danese è finito nel mirino per via della sua doppia funzione: costruire in modo “silenzioso” – Oliver non è un centrale alla John Stones, capace di farsi notare anche a centrocampo -, e sfruttare il mancino sia da braccetto sia da centrale puro. Questione di impatto: la Lazio imposta a tre, con Belahyane che arretra sulla linea dei difensori, e Provstgaard gioca quasi sempre sul centro sinistra. È il primo biancoceleste per passaggi nella trequarti offensiva (85) e primo per passaggi totali".

#2262. Arsenal star Max Dowman smashes Jude Bellingham's record - but a less-familiar name should catch Thomas Tuchel's eye ahead of Euro 2028: THINGS WE LEARNED from England U21's defeat to Portugal

Published: 16:46 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 18:41 EST, 13 November 2026

Max Dowman smashed Jude Bellingham's record on an icy cold night in Bristol to become the youngest player to ever play for England's Under-21s.

The 16-year-old was sent on with 30 minutes to go by Lee Carsley in a move that energised an Ashton Gate crowd that went home frustrated after a 1-0 defeat inflicted by Afonso Moreira's first half goal.

But this was to be Dowman's night, smashing the previous record set by Bellingham when he made his England Under-21 debut against Kosovo in 2020 aged 17 years, two months and six days old. Dowman does not turn 17 until December 31.

Under-21s boss Lee Carsley has been keen long before this international break to try to shield Dowman from the weight of expectation.

But on he came, replacing the lively Amario Cozier-Duberry with a smile on his face and a quiet confidence that he didn't take long to show off, bursting past two Portuguese markers before four huddled together to gang up on him.

Breaking records is nothing new to a player that already holds one for being the Premier League's youngest-ever goalscorer, thanks to his goal against Everton last season.

Max Dowman wrote his name into the record books once more, playing for England Under-21s

Dowman came on to rich applause on a cold night in Bristol to shatter Jude Bellingham's record

The Arsenal teenager was lively playing off the right as he now targets the 2027 U21 Euros

Dowman is both the youngest player to ever win the Premier League and his outing in the Champions League against Slavia Prague last season saw the then-15-year-old become the youngest-ever player to appear in that competition.

By the time next summer's European Championships, which England will attempt to win for a third successive time, rolls around, rising star Dowman could well have earned the right to be this team's main man.

'We've only got three games left before the Euros [next summer],' Carsley explained.

'So it's important that he puts himself in the shop window to be on the plane.

'It's important that he keeps playing well, getting minutes, showing a really good attitude, like he has done.

'I thought he was outstanding when he came on tonight. I thought all of the subs made a real impact.

'I thought Max was unlucky not to get an assist... he made a great pass to Mikey Moore.

'He took up some really good positions, both in the 10 and out wide, he's got a big future ahead.'

His immense talent was one reason why Arsenal sent Ethan Nwaneri, who Dowman played alongside for part of his cameo, to Marseille on loan, and then similarly to Borussia Dortmund, eager not to block the 16-year-old's pathway.

Carsley knows the diamond he has on his hands and will want to take great care in helping polish a player that this crowd, this England team and this management group know has genuine world class potential.

Speaking of Nwaneri, this felt like a big camp for him. A big camp in the sense that he's falling down the pecking order at Dortmund and is back with a manager that has been so vital in his development.

Nwaneri was part of that 2025 group that won the Euros in front of Thomas Tuchel and he'll have seen the likes of Elliot Anderson and Alex Scott kick on to get to the seniors.

As previously mentioned, Nwaneri was moved out of the way by Arsenal to aid Dowman's development, not an easy pill for a player as talented as he is to stomach when his own development journey is at such a critical juncture given he is, to the surprise of many, still only 19.

'I think we have to remember his age as well,' Carsley added when asked about Nwaneri by Daily Mail Sport.

'He's very young is Ethan and he's another one that we've got a lot of hope for. He's got a lot of potential.'

Ethan Nwaneri was pushed to the side at Arsenal to benefit Dowman but England is different

Lee Carsley has been a crucial figure in the development of Nwaneri and this camp was timely

It was telling that even when Dowman came on, even with the groundswell of excitement at his introduction, it was Carsley's call to keep Nwaneri in his preferred No 10 position with Dowman slotting in on the right.

Nwaneri has only started four games on loan at Dortmund this season and has not managed a single start in the Champions League.

His close control, his quick burst over five yards and the fluidity of his movement was a timely reminder that yes, Dowman is the rising star everyone came out to see, but Nwaneri won't get pushed to the side again without a fight.

Amario Cozier-Duberry is a name not all will be familiar with but come next summer, with the Euros in his sights and promotion to the Premier League on the agenda with Middlesbrough, that could all be about to change.

The 21-year-old has had to be very patient, waiting until last month to make his debut at this level after years of finding himself overlooked.

Here, in what was just his third Under-21 appearance, he showed over the course of 61 minutes why the days of being looked past should be over.

One of the big frustrations in the FA right now at Under-21 level is the lack of touchline wingers. Rio Ngumoha, still only 18, has never been capped at the level and has instead been catapulted into the first team, while many, like Ethan Nwaneri, prefer playing inside.

'The more we can get our wide players one v one the better,' Carsley said. 'I think sometimes we leave them isolated and the opposition are able to double up on them.

Amario Cozier-Duberry is not a household name at international level but his talent is immense

'It's important that our game plan can't just be to get the ball wide and hope for the best.'

Dowman, on in place of Cozier-Duberry is a 10, rather than a right winger which is where he came on, and so a player like Cozier-Duberry, who is on loan at Middlesbrough from Brighton & Hove Albion, is worth his weight in gold.

Here in Bristol he was routinely drawing two defenders and at times three; Portugal knew full well how a quick flip-flap of the ball, or one clipped pass over the top, was enough for the Englishman to take the top off the back-line.

His loan at Bolton Wanderers last season complicated an international journey that has seen him play at Under-16, Under-17, Under-18, Under-19 and Under-20 level, but that's all in the past.

England need a genuine, pacy threat out wide if they are to win a third straight Euros. Cozier-Duberry showed here exactly why he should be it.

#2261. Zola: 'Malagò per primo ha avuto fiducia nella riforma, Galliani riconosce il lavoro in Serie C'

(ANSA) - MILANO, 19 NOV - "Io devo essere sincero, devo ringraziare Malagò perché ha avuto fiducia in primis per quello che stiamo facendo in C, mi ha dato un incarico importante per quanto riguarda la ristrutturazione dei nostri settori giovanili. Mi fa piacere che anche Galliani nel suo programma evidenzi il lavoro che la Serie C sta facendo. Significa che c'è comunque comunione di intenti in questo, si è capito che questo è il nostro percorso e lo bisogna fare. Sono contento". Lo dice il vicepresidente vicario della Lega Pro, Gianfranco Zola, parlando delle elezioni federali del 14 dicembre.

"Vediamo quello che succederà - aggiunge Zola, a margine dell'evento a Sky 'Serie C Next Step' -, ciò non toglie che comunque bisogna continuare a concentrarsi ognuno nel proprio lavoro. Il mio è quello di portare avanti questi progetti in modo tale che continuiamo a crescere. La riforma porta il mio nome ma il lavoro è di tutti". Malagò nel suo programma elettorale ha evidenziato di avere rifinanziato la 'riforma Zola' con 5 milioni di euro. (ANSA).

#2260. Lazio, Mimmo Cugini: "Gattuso tira fuori il massimo, la squadra adesso viaggia forte"

Il giornalista della Gazzetta dello Sport analizza il momento d'oro dei biancocelesti: dai meriti di Gattuso al confronto con le big.

Il noto giornalista della Gazzetta dello Sport Mimmo Cugini, intervenuto ai microfoni di TuttoMercatoWeb durante la trasmissione Maracanà, ha analizzato il momento straordinario della Serie A, concentrandosi in particolare sulle ambizioni e sul percorso di crescita della Lazio guidata da Gattuso.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Se facciamo una valutazione tecnica, nelle seconde scelte c'è un gap con le squadre che lottano per il titolo. Bisogna però valutare come gioca questa Lazio, perché Gattuso è riuscito a far dare ai giocatori tutto ciò che hanno e anche qualcosa in più. L'esempio più lampante è quello di Frattesi, che all'Inter era in un sistema di gioco che non lo valorizzava nonostante i gol pesanti segnati in Champions League. I giocatori della Lazio fanno quello che sanno fare e quindi ad ora viaggiano.

Lo scudetto è una grande scommessa e illusione, ma capisco l'entusiasmo dei tifosi, perché una stagione in cui le altre non danno il cento per cento può capitare. Ad ora non vedo troppe squadre che possono metterla in difficoltà, ma sicuramente capiremo di più quando avrà affrontato Roma, Inter e Como. In questo momento sono sulle ali dell'entusiasmo.

#2258. Lots to work on but Pocognoli firmly believes Scotland ARE on the right track under his stewardship

Published: 18:44 EST, 13 November 2026 | Updated: 19:02 EST, 13 November 2026

Scotland boss Sebastien Pocognoli admitted his side were far from perfect in their Nations League win over North Macedonia – but he believes they ARE moving in the right direction under his stewardship.

The victory puts the Scots one point behind second-placed Slovenia in Group B1, ahead of Monday’s trip to runaway leaders Switzerland.

John McGinn notched a second-half double to secure a hard-earned second win of this campaign and Pocognoli’s first in front of the Hampden crowd.

‘We knew before the game that it [a win] will help to build a winning culture,’ he said. ‘And would make the work through the week end with a good feeling.

‘In terms of what we produced on the pitch, I know exactly what we worked on this week. I’m quite satisfied about what I see in some moments of the game, about the work on the ball and off the ball.

‘It was not always perfect but we created a lot of chances that we should score and maybe have a better feeling of the game.

Sebastien Pocognoli got his Hampden win as Scots boss after a 2-1 win over North Macedonia

Stalwarts John McGinn (left) and Andy Robertson applaud the Hampden crowd at full-time

‘Off the ball, I think we were quite aggressive. We recover a number of balls that we managed well in some moments of transition.

‘We created a big chance for Billy [Gilmour] from this also. So that’s a new approach of the game.

‘We created chances and we had a lot of pressure in the side of Macedonia. After the first goal I think we need to learn not to drop the intensity. Because after the first goal for five minutes I really feel like we dropped the intensity and the focus. So, it’s something that we need to fix.’

Asked whether his side need to be more clinical, Pocognoli added: ‘Yes, probably. But I prefer to talk about this with creating a lot of chances. We should score two or three. And probably we should have kept a clean sheet. So that’s really the critical aspect and the demanding aspect.

‘But it’s part of the challenge and we are early in the challenge to try to fix this for the future.’

Talisman McGinn put the Scots ahead on 54 minutes before Elmin Rastoder headed the visitors level against the run of play shortly after, with McGinn then stepping up to convert what turned out to be the winning penalty after a foul on Ben Gannon-Doak with 16 to play.

The Aston Villa midfielder has now taken his tally to 24 for the international team – just six behind legends Kenny Dalglish and Denis Law.

His manager is confident that it is only a matter of time before the 32-year-old is standing alone as his nation’s top scorer, and believes McGinn will have plenty of opportunities to smash the record playing in his team.

John McGinn scored from the penalty spot to clinch an unconvincing victory

He said: ‘I’m more focused on the collective challenge and goals. And I believe if we can build something nice around the collective, then John will one day break the record.

‘We created a lot of chances through these five [Nations League] games together. The individual record is important, but I always focus on the collective package.’

McGinn admitted that chasing the record is in the back of his mind and stressed his relief that he’s back among the goals after a lean spell at international level.

‘It’s been something that’s been in the back of my head, but obviously the results haven’t been the way we’ve wanted,’ said McGinn.

‘The team come first, but whether it’s myself, Scottie (McTominay), Che (Adams), anyone that’s going on the scoresheet at the minute, we’ll take it.

‘It’s been a bit of a dry patch for me over the past year or two on the goal front, so I’m delighted to get back on that trail and hopefully the same again Monday.

‘I feel good. I’ve still got many years to go, but the first priority is getting Scotland back to another major tournament and we’ll take it from there.’
